18 Jan 1920DANIELS' BLIGHT ON NAVY IN WAR THREATENED RUIN OF ALLIES, SIMS CHARGES: Inaction Cost Many Lives and Countless Ships. REVELATIONS ARE SHOCK WARNED AGAINST ENGLISH Senate Committee Halts Probe; Will Ask Broader Powers. Navy Department Told Sims "We Would As Soon Fight British As Germans"--Commander Overseas Was Refused Men and Ships Needed, He Declares-Department Delayed Victory by Refusing to Accept Plans Worked Out in Council With the Allies. Revelations Halt Proceedings. "Terrible," McCormick's Comment. Reads "Camouflage" Cables. Daniels Makes No Comment. Warned Against the British. DANIELS' BLIGHT ON NAVY IN WAR THREATENED RUIN OF ALLIES, SIMS CHARGES Admiral Pleaded in Vain. Ran War From Washington. Won in Spite of Daniels. Needless Losses Resulted. Admiral Cites, the Dispatches.Fox, AW

20 Feb 1921CAN SWIFT AIRPLANES DROPPING BOMBS PUT BA TTLESHIPS OUT OF ACTION? YES, SAYS GEN. MITCHELL; NO, SAY NAVY MEN. TEST MAY DECIDE CONTROVERSY: Attack by Fliers on Radio-Controlled Capital Ship Would Result in Approximately Battle Conditions. Airmen Now Have Monster Bombs of High Explosive. Says He Is Ready to Prove It. "Not Taking Advice Seriously." Reports of Bombing Differ. Vessel Started to Fill. Air Experts Think Otherwise. Would Break All lights. Say Indiana Was Obsolete. No Limit to Size of Bomb. "Cowlike Maneuvers." Jutland as an Example. Britain Sticks to Ships. But Britain Builds Fliers. Only Adjuncts' of Fleet. Hit From Air Difficult. Experts Cannot Agree On Results Achieved By Bombing Indiana Superior Speed of Plane. Fear No Antiaircraft Fire. Collective Bombing. AIRSHIPS VS. BATTLESHIPS Invitation to Disaster. 1 Nov 1921Will fix Navy's size. Harding to review 1923 bill before it goes to Congress. Calls for over $400,000,000. Measure to be important factor at armament conference. Provides for personnel of 126,000 men - Fleets deficient in auxiliary vessels. Despite merchant marine expenditures - Joint Board plans for pacific fortification regarded as significantBrown, GR
2 Nov 1921Halts Navy program. Congress plans not to take up 1923 bill until February. Waits arms adjournment. Leaders to shape policy, no matter what Conference does. Declare any stand the Executive Branch of Government takes will not be considered as limiting Congress' powers - call for economy - Interest centering in protection in the PacificBrown, GR
